            that residue costs instead of hiding it in a spin.
    Gate    Host: 59 checks on the ring, 70 on the present.  Image: 92 / 92,
            seven of them structural rows over the seam.  On target: three
            [GSTS] gif: windows, drop=0 in all of them and one reading
            `queued=48 inflight=1` at the end of a pass.  The refusal path
            is a HOST claim -- SKIP on the guest, where nothing has yet been
            slow enough to reach a capacity 10x a frame.

  LANE 11  a real Tier-1 tick -- a period that is also a deadline.
    Close   VIOLATION 3.  Per-task stacks and preemption at the boundary,
            so a Tier-1 task's budget is enforceable rather than aspirational.
            Its baseline landed on 2026-10-13 and is NOT this lane: the two
            worker planes are now charged against ASYNC_INPUT_BUDGET_US and
            ASYNC_UI_BUDGET_US on every pass and the misses are counted
            (2.1, 2.2, 3), so the budget is already a number this machine can
            miss -- 10 of 12 presenting passes did, 28x over at the worst,
            while the input worker sat at 12 us of its 500.  What lane 11
            still owes is the boundary that turns the count into a bound.
            Until then the honest description is ONE PREEMPTION POINT PER
            FRAME, CHOSEN BY THE BODY (1.4).

  LANE 12  VU1 / VIF1 as a geometry actor.
    Close   the per-vertex arithmetic that row 7 moved onto COP1 but still
            pays the EE for.  The sentence this cell used to end with -- "at a
            few thousand vertices the EE-side conversion is enough, so this lane
            buys headroom, not the frame" -- is RETIRED by measurement, and the
            retirement is the 2026-10-14 eegs companion of ch. 10's stage table.
            Two counts must be kept apart to read the price at all: the mesh
            feeds the pipeline 3,424 unique vertices (4 + 1,984 + 1,436 over the
            steady frame's three stages) and the recorder receives 7,470, because
            prims=2,490 triangles arrive three to a prim.  The EE spends 37,619
            us on the first number -- 11.0 us a vertex, and the SAME 11.0 under
            both backends, which is what makes it the transform's price and not
            the fill's -- against 3,879 us for the send of the second.  The
            front end is 21x the sender, and geometry is 41.6% of a 90,534 us
            pass: the frame's largest single term, on a backend that has no
            pixels left to blame.  A lane that owns 42% of the frame does not
            buy headroom, it buys the frame, and the port's own 5x bar cannot be
            met with this term standing (9d's partition says the other 40% is
            already-refused canvas work, so the two together are the pass).

            What has NOT changed is this lane's COST side, and the difference
            between the two halves of it is the next measurement rather than a
            second argument.  VU1 needs a micro-program, and this tree has no VU
            assembler and no VU row in any log to charge one against -- that part
            of Fact 3 still holds, and a lane cannot be taken by wanting it.  But
            the 11.0 us is a sum of four things the EE does itself (transform,
            viewport map, clip, and the vertex record's load/store traffic), and
            the instrument that splits it is already compiled in: PS2_FP_COUNT=1
            makes the [XMBT] census rows bill a stage's float calls in seven
            classes, and the fitted per-class prices turn that census into
            microseconds without touching the source.  Read under eegs, where
            fill is structurally zero, the census row of f_ribbon is the whole of
            its geometry bill in floats.  That row has now been RUN, on the
            shipping dials with the census dial on, 2026-10-14:

              [FPU] 16000-op soft-float caller: COP1 34 cycles/op  bit engine 558
              [FPU] conversion price: float->int 53 (4000 calls)  int->float 29
              census f_ribbon fmul=28881 fdiv=248 fcmp=4693 fs2i=17852 fi2s=18964
                     S=70638 dmul=0 dgen=0 D=0        stage 26,745 us
              census f_menu   fmul=13628 fdiv=1470 fcmp=1580 fs2i=5038 fi2s=11093
                     S=32809                          stage 13,077 us
              census f_surface fmul=5163 fdiv=39 fcmp=1432 fs2i=1027 fi2s=1
                     S=7662 dmul=930 dgen=775 D=1705  stage  8,454 us

            Priced class by class at the numbers above -- arith, div and compare
            at 34 cycles, float->int at 53, int->float at 29, every double at the
            bit engine's 558 -- f_ribbon's 70,638 calls are 2,646,060 cycles =
            8,970 us, which is 33.5% of a stage the paragraph above priced at 12.7
            us a vertex.  f_menu's 32,809 calls are 3,918 us of 13,077, 30%.  f_surface's
            are 4,174 us of 8,454, 49%, and 3,225 us of that is its 1,705 DOUBLES:
            the wave's own surface step still reaching include/gl/math.h's double
            series, at five times what every single-precision call in the same
            stage costs.  The three geometry stages of one frame therefore carry
            17,062 us of float runtime calls -- 15% of a 111,731 us period -- and
            the other 85% of geometry is the integer walk, the clip, and the
            packet's load/store traffic.  So the answer to the question this cell
            asked is the FIRST branch, with a residue: the fix is COP1's calling
            convention, the EE keeps the lane, and VU1 is not what a third of the
            term is waiting for.  The residue is the other two thirds; it has since
            been measured rather than named, and the measurement is the RUN result
            under 12a and 12b.

            Where the calls are is a static fact about the linked image -- its own
            `jal` targets, read off `llvm-objdump -d btron-ps2.elf` -- and it sorts
            into three rungs, each with its own gate:

  LANE 12a  the vertex packet, in integers.  clip.c's gs_vertex() holds four
            __floatsisf and two __mulsf3 per vertex -- `(float)p->x`, `(float)p->y`,
            `(float)p->s * GS_TEX_U`, `(float)p->t * GS_TEX_V` -- over a ZBufferPoint
            whose x, y, s and t are ALREADY GLint, and ps2_gs_rec.c's gs_rec_vertex()
            answers by calling gs_vertex_coord16() twice, each of which is one
            __mulsf3 and one __fixsfsi: `(int32_t)(px * 16.0f)`.  The image thus
            converts an integer to a float, multiplies that float by 16.0f in soft
            float, and converts the product back to the integer it started from --
            eight runtime calls per vertex to do a shift.  It is not an
            approximation that replaces an exact step: x * 16 is representable in
            binary32 for every |x| < 2^20, the device pixels on this path are under
            4,096, and truncating an integer-valued float returns that integer, so
            the two spellings return the same word by routes of different length.
            The texel scale is a power of two by construction (GS_TEX_U is
            1/(1 << (ZB_POINT_S_FRAC_BITS + 1))) and gs_pack_st() wants only its
            BITS, so it folds into the exponent field as `bits - (k << 23)` with
            zero named as the case that must not be shifted.  The price is 8 calls
            x 1,984 ribbon vertices = 15,872 calls = 2.0 ms a frame, plus the same
            per primitive in f_menu and f_bg.  GATE: the packet bytes, bit for bit,
            against the float spelling over a sweep of vertices, in
            test_ps2_gs_rec and test_ps2_gs_pack.

  LANE 12b  kill the clipper's second viewport map.  clip.c's
            gl_transform_to_viewport_clip_c() is a verbatim duplicate of
            vertex_stage.c's tgl_vertex_to_viewport() -- the same three projection
            lines, the same four colour lines, the same two texture lines,
            differing only in writing `(GLint)` where the other writes
            `TGL_TRUNC()` -- and it is the copy that lives in the -msoft-float unit.
            Its 31 call sites (9 __fixsfsi, 12 __mulsf3, 9 __addsf3, 1 __divsf3) run
            once per vertex the clipper creates, and the census counts them
            exactly: fdiv=248 is one divide per call, so 248 clipped vertices,
            7,688 calls, 0.98 ms.  One truth is the law here as much as the saving:
            the copy that survives is the -mhard-float one, reached by pointer,
            which is the boundary row 7 already made legal, and both spellings
            truncate through the same ps2_sf_fixsfsi(), so no answer moves when the
            duplicate dies.

            Both rungs have been RUN, 2026-10-14, on the shipping dials
            (PS2_GL=eegs PS2_FP=hw PS2_MSX=0, BENCH_APP=1, PS2_FP_COUNT=0) against
            the image of the code before them; the two logs are
            build/ps2-bench-9c-baseline.log and build/ps2-bench-A-prod.log, and
            every row below is frame 1 / cycle 1 of the same three-frame run:

              f_ribbon geometry   25,168 -> 21,849 us  (1,984 verts: 12.7 -> 11.0 us)
              f_menu  geometry    12,340 -> 11,261 us  (1,436 verts:  8.6 ->  7.8 us)
              f_surface            8,253 ->  8,248 us  (unmoved, as designed)
              frame 2 draw=       46,864 -> 42,483 us
              cycle 1 pass=       90,534 -> 86,153 us
              cycle 1 render=     85,764 -> 81,386 us
              comp total=         76,754 -> 72,370 us
              comp bg/frame/blit   7,484/7,851/12,654 -> 7,480/7,853/12,661 us

            The win is the whole of the draw term and none of the composite's: the
            two stages the rungs touch account for 3,319 + 1,050 = 4,369 of the
            4,381 us draw= fell by, and the three composite rows that no rung
            touches are the same number to within their noise.  Priced before they
            ran, a and b were 2.0 + 0.98 = 2.98 ms; the measured 4.38 ms is 1.46x
            that, and the census run on the same tree says where the difference is
            -- with PS2_FP_COUNT=1,

              f_ribbon  S=70,638 -> 34,442  (fs2i 17,852 -> 5,952, fi2s 18,964 -> 7,932,
                                             fmul 28,881 -> 15,745, fcmp 4,693 -> 4,689)
              f_menu    S=32,809 -> 18,995  (fs2i  5,038 -> 1,090, fi2s 11,093 -> 7,217)

            so 50,010 calls a frame left the image, 15,848 of them float -> int and
            14,908 int -> float: the round trip 12a named, concentrated in the two
            classes whose prices (53 and 29 cycles) are the highest of the singles,
            which is why deleting 12a's eight calls a vertex returns more than the
            34-cycle arith price of eight calls would say.  Read the other way the
            same census is the lane's control on itself: multiplying these counts
            by the [FPU] prices gives 6.5 ms where the A/B measured 4.38, because
            the counter's load, add and store sit inside the interval the priced
            loop measured -- so this book quotes the production row for the
            microseconds and uses the census only for ATTRIBUTION.  Two things only
            the census settles.  The fdiv column HALVED (248 -> 124) rather than
            emptied, because the viewport map that survives does its own
            `1.0 / v->pc.W` for every vertex it maps: one divide per clipped vertex
            is the truth's work and not a duplicate's, and 12b is done when the
            count is halved, not when it is zero.  And fcmp fell by four in a stage
            of 1,984 vertices, which says neither rung touched a comparison -- the
            exact signature the two paragraphs predicted, since both convert and
            multiply and neither tests.

  LANE 12c  the clip's own lerps, now PRICED, and priced small.  What is left
            after a and b is 34,442 of f_ribbon's 70,638 calls: updateTmp(),
            GLinterpolate() and the six clip_proc() functions, each a handful of
            float add and mul over pc, colour and texcoord, plus the triangle
            normal's four __floatsisf and two __mulsf3 per triangle.  Their prices
            are known, and the stage census now bills them -- the paragraph above
            reads their post-12a/b total -- so the rung's ceiling is computable
            without writing the per-function instrument this cell originally asked
            for: 34,442 calls at 34/53/29 cycles is ~4 ms of a 21.8 ms stage and
            ~3.9% of a 102 ms period.  Decision recorded here: leave 12c written
            and unfunded.  The bar is 5x, and 3.9% of a frame is not something a
            hand on the ribbon can see.

            What no census sees is the rest of the stage, and after a and b the
            rest is most of it.  f_ribbon costs 21,849 us for 1,984 vertices; ~4 ms
            of that is its float calls and 3,878 us is the GIF posting the [GSTS]
            row names, so ~14 ms -- 2,000 cycles a vertex -- is integer work this
            port has never billed: the immediate-mode vertex copy, the strip's
            per-vertex dispatch through glBegin/glVertex/glEnd, the clip-plane
            tests per triangle, and the recorder's own stores.  The prize is that
            number, so the next rung is arithmetic about vertices, not about
            floats:

  LANE 12d  stop paying 2,000 cycles to hand the GS one vertex.  The PRE-GATE
            ran, both halves static against the shipping ELF, and it killed both
            rungs -- which is what a pre-gate is for, and cheaper here by one
            bench run.
            (i) THE MESH: closed by this book's own chapter 10, which this cell
            re-opened without reading.  32x32 -> 16x16 is worth the ~4x it
            claims and costs 10.81% of the sheet's pixels with a worst channel
            delta of 145/255, and verify/tests/test_xmb_render.c refuses
            n < 32*32 as a law.  A 145/255 error is not a fidelity trade an eye
            gets to make; it is a different surface.  The sentence asking the
            user to choose was wrong and is withdrawn.
            (ii) THE PATH: statically refuted.  glDrawArrays (arrays.c:361) is
            glBegin, a loop of glArrayElement, glEnd; glArrayElement only pushes
            OP_ArrayElement, and glopArrayElement (arrays.c:310) ends by calling
            glopVertex -- llvm-objdump of the linked image, 0x12b398..0x12b5a8,
            exactly one `jal 0x136cc0 <glopVertex>`.  That is the same handler
            the immediate path's dispatch reaches, so the two halves of the
            pre-gate answer themselves with one verdict: the array path shares
            the vertex funnel, hence the same draw_triangle hook and a
            byte-identical stream, AND for the same reason it does no less work.
            It adds an op enqueue plus the index arithmetic per vertex.  A path
            that cannot differ from the one it replaces cannot be cheaper.
            Closed; no second encoding to kill, because there was never a second
            one.

            What the walk did find is where 12d's ~14 ms lives, and it is not
            dispatch.  Instruction counts straight off nm -S of the shipping
            image -- static sizes, so read them per call on the taken path, not
            as a bill: glVertex2f 33, glopVertex 240, gl_draw_triangle 205,
            eegs_triangle 118, gs_rec_vertex 462 (that 462 is three inlined
            clones of the emit block, one per split branch; the path a ribbon
            vertex takes is a little under half).  The decisive number is in the
            [GSTS] row instead: verts=7,467 prims=2,489.  That is 3.00 vertices
            per primitive, exactly -- the shape of this port's seam, not of the
            scene.  backend_eegs.c:150 calls emit_vertex() three times per
            triangle, so the ribbon's 1,984 strip vertices arrive as 1,838
            triangles and 5,514 vertex packets, every one of them re-stating a
            PRIM, an RGBAQ and an ST the GS already holds.

  LANE 12e  draw the strip the recorder already knows.  The GS does not want
            three vertices a triangle for a strip; it wants two to start and one
            after that, and it alternates the winding itself.  ps2_gs_rec.c
            implements that primitive already -- can_split() at :124 and
            reemit_continuation() at :138 are written for TRI_STRIP and TRI_FAN
            continuation, and the comment at :135 even reasons about the parity
            a restarted strip loses.  The backend never asks for it: it builds
            every triangle as GS_OP_TRI.  So the rung is at the seam, not in the
            recorder: vertex.c's STRIP case hands the hook the same stored
            c->vertex[] objects the previous call used for its shared edge, and
            the backend needs to know that this triangle's v0,v1 are the last
            triangle's v1,v2, unmodified, so it can select TRI_STRIP once and
            feed ONE vertex per triangle after the first two.
            The precondition is checked, not assumed: the shared vertices are
            identical only while the clipper has not cut, so a split triangle, a
            PRIM change, an AD write (gs_rec_ad invalidates the live PRIM for
            exactly this reason) or a failed compare falls back to the present
            three-packet path, and the fallback is counted so a silent loss of
            the win is visible in the row that reports it.
            MEASURE, first: the [GSTS] verts/prims ratio, 3.00 -> 1.08 on a
            ribbon frame, and f_ribbon's us column against 21,912.  A third of
            the recorder's work staying on the table would be an honest result
            and a visible one; the claim is that it goes to ~4% of its size on
            the shared vertices, because the vertices stop being sent.

            RUN, 2026-10-14, on the shipping dials (PS2_GL=eegs PS2_FP=hw), with
            the instrument this rung's own fallback clause asked for compiled in
            as the `chain=/entry= miss=/fresh=/pchg=` columns, and it says the
            rung is PARKED: the ratio moved, the microseconds did not, and the
            price landed on the picture instead --

              verts/prims   7,467/2,489 = 3.00  ->  6,435/2,489 = 2.59
              chain=516/223 miss=223 fresh=1,750 pchg=470   (next frame 424/232
              miss=232 fresh=1,824 pchg=488; the three exits plus the list path
              partition `prims` exactly, 516+223+1,750 = 2,489)
              groups           51 -> 256  =  the EEGS_MAX_GROUPS table, full
              drop                0 -> 236  primitives the app drew and the
                                         screen does not have
              gs=            3,874 -> 4,025 us  (it did NOT get cheaper)
              f_ribbon      22,701 -> 21,842 us, and the parked build
                                          reads 21,919: the run-to-run
                                          spread is 4%, as wide as any
                                          saving this rung could claim

            The mechanism is in the counters, and it is not the geometry: 516
            of the frame's 2,489 primitives found a strip they could
            continue -- and the ribbon alone draws 1,838 of those primitives,
            so the chain rate is at most 28% of the ribbon -- and a miss is
            not one event but two: the list path that follows it, then the
            next triangle's entry back into a strip, which is why `miss=` and
            `entry=` are equal and why 223 of them put `pchg=` at 470 against
            the parked build's 24.  A group is the unit the recorder charges
            for, `pchg=` is its rate, and this rung multiplied that rate 20x
            -- 51 groups into a 256-entry table, capped, 236 primitives
            dropped -- to save 14% of the vertices; and the frame the user
            watches did not change, which is the observation that opened this
            measurement.

            PARKED: eegs_triangle() asks for GS_OP_TRI only again, drop= returns
            to 0, and the four chain counters go with the branch they measured --
            the instrument that outlives them is `pchg=` against `groups=`, the
            group-boundary rate and its budget -- EEGS_MAX_GROUPS=256, which the
            static assert against PS2_GIF_RING_CAP=512 in eegs_present.c refuses
            to let anyone raise without touching LANE 10's law -- and the
            verts/prims ratio in the
            same row is how a re-lived rung shows up without any new counter.
            What would make this rung worth taking again is a group that
            survives a PRIM change: the GS carries PRIM as a per-vertex register,
            so a stream CAN state it mid-group, and the recorder's strip
            machinery is not deleted for that reason -- can_split()'s TRISTP arm
            and reemit_continuation() stay gated and tested in
            test_ps2_gs_rec.c, where the primitive itself is under test rather
            than the backend's decision to ask for it.  12f's batch entry has no
            such change to make, because it opens ONE group per mesh.

            VU1 stays the lane's other half: unbuilt, un-priced, still no
            assembler and still no row.

  LANE 12f  one draw call for a mesh the port already has in hand.  What this
            lane is priced against, with 12e's park taken and the stage read
            again: f_ribbon is 21,842 us, 21,919 us and 22,701 us in the three
            bench runs this session retained, for the 1,984 vertices the ribbon
            submits -- so take ~21.9 us x 1,000, or 11.0 us = 3,260 cycles a
            vertex at the dialled 295 cycles/us, and treat the 4% spread as the
            resolution limit any claim here has to clear.  Its float bill is
            34,442 calls, and this book's
            per-class prices are now validated in situ rather than fitted: the
            two runs either side of 12a and 12b moved 36,196 calls and 4,896 us,
            and 11,900 float->int at 53 + 11,032 int->float at 29 + 13,136 arith
            at 34 predicts 4.74 ms of that -- 3% off.  So the same 34,442
            remaining calls are ~4.7 ms, 21% of the stage, and making every
            float in the ribbon's path hard-float is a 1.3x on one stage and
            1.1x on the frame.  The other 17 ms is ~2,600 cycles a vertex of
            INTEGER work: the immediate-mode walk the 12d cell counted --
            glVertex2f 33 + glopVertex 240 + gl_draw_triangle 205 +
            eegs_triangle 118 + the emit block, over a 4-stage in-order pipe
            that overlaps nothing a dependent load is waiting on.

            The bar is 5x on the surface the user watches.  No amount of
            arithmetic on that walk reaches it, because the walk itself -- not
            any step inside it -- is the term: the ribbon's geometry is ALREADY
            in this port's own arrays (xb_rib_px, xb_rib_py, xb_rib_bri,
            XB_RIBBON_ROWS x XB_RIBBON_COLS = 32x32 floats each, written this
            frame by xmb_wave.c on COP1), and it is
            then re-derived a thousand times a second through a general-purpose
            immediate-mode pipeline that exists to serve callers who do NOT have
            their mesh in hand.  So the rung is to stop paying for generality
            where the data is retained: a batch entry that takes the grid's
            arrays, the row/col counts and the state (blend, smooth, no
            texture), and writes the strip's quadwords into the recorder
            directly -- fixed-point map, colour byte pack, one tag back-patch at
            the end -- for ~150-250 cycles a vertex against ~2,600.  The cell's
            claim is 22 ms -> ~2 ms on f_ribbon, which is the first change in
            this lane large enough to be the thing the user asked for; the price
            of NOT believing it is one bench run.

            What the walk actually computes per vertex, read off the pipeline it
            walks -- these are the facts the batch entry reproduces, not
            approximations of them:
            - the projection is `tgl_ortho(0,w,h,0,-1,1)` (backend_tinygl.c)
              over an identity modelview, so pc.W is exactly 1.0f, and the
              `winv = 1.0 / pc.W` divide returns exactly 1.0f -- it does not
              have to be re-done, only proven to be one;
            - pc.X is fl(fl(x*m0) + m3) with m0 = 2/w and m3 = -1, because the
              matrix's other three terms contribute exact zeros; likewise
              pc.Y = fl(fl(y*m5) + m7);
            - x_px = TRUNC(fl(fl(pc.X * winv) * scale.X) + trans.X), which is
              vertex_stage.c's own association order, and TRUNC is that file's
              32-bit truncate rather than a cast;
            - the colour is grey (glColor4f(b,b,b,1)), so ONE
              TRUNC(b*0xfe0000 + 0x00ffff) serves r, g and b, and the alpha byte
              is a constant of the draw.
            Ten float ops and three truncates a vertex, over the 1,984 points a
            32x32 grid's zigzag submits ((ROWS-1)*COLS*2), is ~0.4 ms of the
            stage where the walk bills ~17.  The float SEMANTICS are why the
            map loop belongs in vertex_stage.c -- the `-mhard-float` TU that
            already owns the map -- and not in a soft-float TU: C permits a
            float expression in a soft-float unit to be carried in double
            intermediates, which is not the arithmetic being matched, and this
            port has already been black-screened once by that difference.

            Rungs, each with its own gate:
            (i) the batch entry, gated HOST-side on two separate claims,
                neither of them an identity between two copies of one code:
                (a) the VALUES.  `tgl_grid_map()` lifts the four constants the
                    above list names out of the live context, and for every
                    swept (x, y, b) the batch's arithmetic must land on the same
                    GLint pixel and the same colour byte as a real
                    `tgl_vertex_to_viewport()` on the same vertex -- in a suite
                    that links tinygl, so the comparator is the map itself and
                    not a restatement of it.  This is the gap 12b's comment
                    names: nothing on the host has checked that a whole-pixel
                    value is the one the map produced.  The same suite owns the
                    REFUSALS, because the two-constant-per-axis form is only
                    this arithmetic under conditions: lighting off, nothing
                    bound-and-enabled on TEXTURE_2D, a diagonal projection, a W
                    that does not depend on the vertex -- and a model-projection
                    product the walk has actually computed.  That last condition
                    is not cosmetic: a matrix change leaves
                    `matrix_model_projection_updated` set and
                    `matrix_model_projection` holding the PREVIOUS product, which
                    is only refreshed at `glBegin`, so a map lifted between a
                    resize and the frame's first `glBegin` would take the ribbon
                    through the old window for exactly one frame and print
                    nothing on the console.  `tgl_grid_map()` refuses that rather
                    than lifting a stale product, and every refusal here is
                    paired with the same state restored and accepted, so a -1
                    cannot be earned by something else the context lacks.
                (b) the STREAM.  For one grid, the strip the batch records must
                    DECODE -- through the recorder's own group layout -- into
                    the same triangle sequence, with the same vertex payloads,
                    that the list path records for the same grid, the zigzag's
                    turn-around included; and a 3x3 grid's group must equal a hand-built
                    expected strip group byte for byte.  A strip is not a list,
                    so "byte for byte" is a claim about the encoding and about
                    each vertex's fields, never about two streams being the same
                    length -- that would be the wrong gate, and it would fail.
            (ii) the clip question, which is NOT assumed either way.  The GS
                 receives a clip rect in its environment packet (the [GSTS] row
                 prints it as `clip=768x500`), but this port has no measured
                 fact yet about what the GS does with a vertex OUTSIDE it, and a
                 fast path that guesses wrong loses part of the picture
                 silently.  So the gate is a bbox: the batch path is taken only
                 when the grid's own bounding box is inside the window's rect,
                 and every other frame takes the present path.  `batch=` and
                 `slow=` go in the row, so the ratio of the two is the evidence
                 that the fast path is not quietly eating an edge of the sheet.
            (iii) the call site in xmb.c, which keeps its GL-visible meaning --
                  same vertices, same colours, same GL_DST_COLOR/GL_ONE
                  compositing -- and is the user's to observe, not mine to
                  declare fixed.

            LANDED on the host 2026-10-14, rungs (i)-(iii), each with the
            control that was supposed to make it fail:
            (a) `verify/tests/test_grid_map.c`, 130 checks / 0 failures
                (121 at this rung's landing, +5 when the refusals got names,
                +5 with the ribbon's own mesh).  It
                links tinygl and drives the real `tgl_vertex_to_viewport()`, so
                the comparator is the walk itself; ~173k swept lattice vertices
                x 6 alphas land on the same GLint pixel and the same colour byte
                through `tgl_grid_map_points()`.  Three controls were run and
                each had to bite: PERTURBING each of the eight lifted map fields
                moves the points (so no field is decorative); computing the map
                in DOUBLE intermediates moves 1,932 / 252 / 246 device pixels,
                all of them boundary probes (so the truncation is measured, not
                assumed); and refusing a context with
                `matrix_model_projection_updated` set -- then accepting it once
                undone -- is what makes the freshness clause load-bearing rather
                than stylistic.  The boundary bracket is five probes per pixel
                column/row, straddling with `nextafterf` at offsets -8..+8 ulp.
                Two claims got written down honestly instead: ASSOCIATION is a
                no-op here because `winv == 1.0f` exactly (re-associating the
                product did NOT fail the suite), and `-ffp-contract=off` in the
                Makefile is insurance for gcc's default -- on Apple clang 21 /
                arm64 the flag measured as not biting.  TRUNCATION is the one
                that bites.  A second hazard the same suite surfaced only on the
                MIPS side: written as one interleaved loop, the body hoists all ten
                of the map's floats and the allocator, past the twenty
                caller-saved singles of the o32 hard-float ABI, moves into
                `$f20/$f22/$f24` -- where saving a *single* costs its even/odd
                pair, so six `sdc1`/`ldc1` appeared in the image and TEST 66 of
                `make test-ps2` failed.  `tgl_grid_map_points()` is three passes
                (x, then y, then colour) instead, five hoisted floats apiece, zero
                such instructions, and the identical 121 checks.  The control was
                run in both directions: the interleaved shape fails, the split
                shape passes, and no value test distinguishes them.
            (b) `verify/tests/test_eegs_raster.c`, 222 checks / 0 failures, with
                `eegs_grid_strip()`'s stream decoded back through the recorder's
                own group layout and compared to the list path for the same
                grid, and a 3x3 group equal to a hand-built expected strip group
                byte for byte.  The gate added here refused on the rect and is
                now the amended one (see rung (iii) below): a mesh that leaves
                the rect is TAKEN and its outside points land in `over=`, while
                a point the vertex word cannot carry refuses with -1 and
                increments `slow=`.  The vertex count the gate walks is
                `rows*cols` -- the mapped points -- not `(rows-1)*cols*2` -- the
                submitted ones -- and the first version of this got that wrong,
                which is how it earned its test.  The four exits' own controls,
                each run by mutation on the same file (2026-10-15): deleting the
                range branch fails 9 checks (both folding points, the y axis,
                and the refusal's empty `over=`); putting the rect back as the
                refusal condition -- the gate exactly as first written, which is
                the build that printed `why=7` for a living -- fails the other 9
                (every taken overhang, on both axes and both edges); and moving
                `over`'s commit inside the scan fails the one check that a
                refusal mid-mesh leaves the column where it found it.  Restored,
                the suite is the 222 above with no warning from the stage.
            (ii) The clip question is measured rather than guessed, in both
                 directions: `compare_clip_gate()` in the grid-map suite sweeps
                 32 probes outside the viewport rect through the real clipper and
                 requires that clip-inside implies inside the integer rect, and
                 that clip-outside never excludes a rect-interior point except
                 inside the sub-pixel band TRUNC's toward-zero rounding leaves --
                 the band the `band` column counts and reports.  Two off-by-one
                 controls (`vp_x1` one short, `vp_y0` one long) both fire, so the
                 rect's coefficients are read, not printed.
            (iii) `xb_draw_ribbon()` in `src/apps/xmb.c` calls the batch entry
                  once per frame under `GL_BACKEND_EEGS` and falls back to
                  `glBegin`/`glEnd` when it returns -1; the host-side gates above
                  say the stream is right, and only the user's monitor says the
                  sheet still waves.  The price of NOT believing the 22 ms -> ~2 ms
                  cell is one bench run, and the row that decides it is
                  `batch=`/`slow=` against `f_ribbon`.

                  The first of those runs answered `batch=0 slow=1` on every frame
                  and `f_ribbon` unmoved at 22,374 us -- and the reason was the
                  ordering this lane's own rung (i)(a) paragraph had already named.
                  The app's `glOrtho`/`glViewport`/`glLoadIdentity` for the wave run
                  five lines above the draw, `matrix.c` only sets
                  `matrix_model_projection_updated` on a matrix change, and it is
                  `glopBegin` that multiplies the stacks and clears the flag; so a
                  map lifted BEFORE the `glBegin` is a map lifted against the
                  PREVIOUS product, and the refusal was correct on every frame of
                  the batch path's first live use.  The call site now asks inside
                  the `glBegin` -- which is the same pair the immediate walk uses,
                  so nothing else about the draw changes, and `glopEnd()` for
                  `GL_TRIANGLE_STRIP` only clears `in_begin`, so an empty strip
                  records nothing twice.  The instrument's verdict: this is what
                  the freshness clause cost, measured rather than argued.

                  The run after that answered `batch=0 slow=1 why=7`, again on
                  every frame, and `f_ribbon` unmoved at 22,282 us.  `why=7` is
                  the rect gate, and the reason it fires is geometry, not state:
                  the ribbon does not fit the rect.  The suite's own ribbon
                  section measures the shipped mesh against the shipped map at
                  the shipped window -- canvas 768x500, which is the row's
                  `clip=768x500`, and projection 776x536, which is its
                  `dmg=12,20,788,556`: `open_xmb_window()` sets `s_w`/`s_h` from
                  the DESK minus the margins (800-24, 600-64) and `xb_paint()`
                  steps the wave with those, while the camera and the surface
                  are the CLIENT area.  Over 2,000 frames of `xb_wave_step()`,
                  the period of the sheet's slowest term, the mapped mesh spans
                  x_px 0..775 and y_px 245..433 against a rect of 0..767 x
                  0..499.  Exactly 32 points leave on every frame, in exactly
                  one column, on exactly one edge -- the mesh's x comes from the
                  column index alone, so the 8 px of overdraw the background
                  quad puts in on purpose (`xb_draw_bg()` corners at `(0,s_h)`
                  and `(s_w,0)`, so no canvas edge shows through) reaches the
                  sheet as a last column that is permanently outside.
                  The control separates that from the wave: the same sheet
                  projected at the canvas size spans 0..767 x 229..404, with
                  nothing outside on any of the 2,000 frames.  So the surface
                  never leaves the window; the mesh is 1% too wide, always.

                  Which asks the wrong question of this stream.  The rect gate
                  was rung (ii)'s answer to "the batch path does not clip", and
                  the clipper's answer is true of the EE -- but the GS is
                  already bounded to the same rect: `eegs_present_send()`
                  programs SCISSOR from the very `client` it is handed
                  (`env.clip_x0..clip_y1`), and XYOFFSET from the same rect's
                  origin, so the app's (0,0) is the client's top-left and a
                  primitive that crosses the edge is a primitive the hardware
                  cuts.  That is what the clipper was doing for ~3,260 cycles a
                  vertex.  So rung (ii)'s gate is amended rather than removed:
                  what a whole-mesh batch must be refused for is a vertex the GS
                  cannot REPRESENT, not one it cannot show, and the field that
                  decides that is XYZ2's 12.4 range about the page origin
                  (`GS_XYOFFSET_PAGE`, ps2_gs_pack.h:110) -- and its extent is
                  -2048 .. +2047, not symmetric by one, because
                  `gs_vertex_coord16()` ADDS the offset to `px * 16` and puts the
                  sum through a 16-bit unsigned field, so -2048 packs to 0x0000
                  and survives while 2048 packs to the same word and does not.
                  The suite found that, not this sentence: the first draft of the
                  gate expected -2048 to refuse and it did not.  A point past the
                  bound folds in the field and lands somewhere else on the canvas;
                  that is the corruption the gate exists for, and it stays a
                  refusal, as `why=9` (`TGL_GRID_REFUSE_RANGE`).  A point
                  between the rect and that bound is taken and counted in the
                  new `over=` column -- the points this frame's batches drew
                  outside the rect, which is what the GS is being asked to
                  clip.  For the ribbon that is 32 a frame, the same 32 the
                  suite names, and the row that decides this change is `over=32`
                  beside a sheet whose right edge is unchanged on the user's
                  monitor.  `why=7` stays in the code as the name that run
                  printed, and is retired from production: nothing makes it now.
                  The failure mode if SCISSOR does not mean what its register
                  says is 8 columns of sheet painted over the window's own
                  border -- visible, bounded, and printed in the row, which is
                  the shape rung (ii) asked for; a silent loss would be the
                  alternative, and the walk cannot offer that and 20 ms.

                  LANDED 2026-10-14, and the row rung (iii) named is this one --
                  identical on every frame of the XMB pass, on the shipping dials
                  (`PS2_GL=eegs PS2_FP=hw PS2_MSX=0 PS2_FP_COUNT=0 BENCH_APP=1`,
                  `scripts/ps2_bench.sh`):

                    [GSTS] groups=47 qw=15869 verts=3937 prims=2633 gs=3773 us |
                    [GSTS]   pchg=24 batch=1 slow=0 why=0 over=32 | drop=0 unsplit=0
                    [GSTS]   texsw=22 texmiss=0 | cover=4 coverdrop=0 |
                    [GSTS]   clip=768x500 b0=0,0,767,499 plate=covered

                  `batch=1 slow=0` on every frame: the mesh is taken, and the walk
                  is not run.  `why=0` beside `slow=0`: no refusal had to be named,
                  which is also the live proof that the retired `why=7` is not
                  merely uncalled but unreachable on this window -- the gate that
                  starved three runs is gone from the path, not from the enum.
                  `over=32` is the suite's 32 produced by the shipped image: the
                  GS is being asked to cut the sheet's last column, and the row
                  says so every frame.  Whether the sheet's right edge is still
                  there while it is asked is the user's observation, and the
                  monitor is the only instrument for it.

                  What moved, against the parked baseline the lane was priced
                  from (the 12e-park row of the same dial, same script, frame 1):

                    f_ribbon        21,842 / 21,919 us  ->  3,522 / 3,517   6.2x
                    draw= frame 1   45,094 us          ->  26,839 us       1.68x
                    cycle period=   102,185 us         ->   86,512 us       1.18x
                    qw=             29,985             ->  15,869         (-47%)
                    verts=           7,467             ->   3,937         (-47%)
                    groups=             51             ->      47
                    verts=/prims=     3.00             ->    1.49
                    pchg=               24             ->      24

                  The last two rows are LANE 12e's question answered by a lane that
                  did not need its columns: a strip that is one draw buys 0.49
                  primitives a vertex instead of a third of one, and `pchg=` -- the
                  group-boundary rate 12e paid 470 to get -- is unmoved, because
                  the ribbon was never the reason a group closes.  `drop=` is 0,
                  so nothing was refused by the budget, and `gs=` is inside the
                  3,864 us the parked frame measured, which is the standing answer
                  to "did a third of the stream just stop being sent while the
                  present paid for it": no.

                  3,517 us over the 1,984 vertices the mesh submits is 523 cycles a
                  vertex at the dialled 295 cycles/us, against the walk's measured
                  3,260 -- so the batch is 6.2x cheaper and this lane's own
                  150-250-cycle estimate was 2-3.5x optimistic.  The estimate was
                  made by counting the map's ten float ops and three truncates;
                  what it did not count is that the batch entry also packs four
                  registers into two quadwords per vertex and keeps the recorder's
                  group bookkeeping, and those three terms are now one number with
                  no instrument separating them.  PROVEN: 523 is the price.
                  INFERRED: that the residue is the pack rather than the map.  The
                  rung that would settle it is the one 12d ran for the walk -- a
                  counted cell inside `eegs_grid_strip()` -- and it is worth having
                  before this entry is reused for a second mesh, which is 12h.

                  The frame's census, which is the thing the reprioritization is
                  about (frame 2; the six stage rows sum to 24,288 against the
                  frame's own 24,319, so the partition is complete):

                    f_menu     11,441 us   47%     f_surface   5,756 us  24%
                    f_ribbon    3,517 us   14%     f_clear     3,408 us  14%
                    f_bg          131 us    1%     f_particles    35 us   0%

                  The sheet's own stage is now 14% of the frame and the menu's is
                  47% of it, and the same frame read from outside the app -- the
                  cycle row, which is the clock the user's eye is actually on --
                  partitions like this:

                    period  83,944 = pass 67,934 + hold 16,001
                    pass    67,934 = render 63,267 + upload 4,653
                    render  63,267 = comp total 54,256 + 9,011 unnamed
                    comp total 54,256 = paint 24,343 + blit 12,671 + frame 7,869
                                        + bg 7,499 + panel 1,568 + bars 296

                  So the app frame is 29% of the period and `f_ribbon` is 4.2% of
                  it: making the wave's own stage 6.2x cheaper moves the rate the
                  user watches by 21%, and this is why the rungs before this one
                  could land with nothing on the monitor to show for them.  The
                  bar's arithmetic has moved too, and the book's own 9d sentence
                  ("90,534 minus 36,154 minus 37,619 leaves 16,761 us", i.e. 9d and
                  12 together are the 5x) must be re-read on today's partition
                  rather than quoted: taking out the whole app frame (24,343) AND
                  everything 9d can refuse (bg 7,499 + blit 12,671 + upload 4,653 =
                  24,823) leaves 18,768 us of pass -- `frame=` 7,869, `panel=` +
                  `bars=` 1,864 and the 9,011 the composite spends outside its own
                  named columns -- which is 4.5x on the period, not 6.4x.  The
                  remaining 2x of the bar is therefore NOT in LANE 12 and NOT in
                  what 9d currently bounds: it is in `frame=` and in the unnamed
                  residue, and neither has an instrument yet.

  LANE 12g  the surface stage's 1,705 doubles, 3,225 us.  Priced in 12c and
            never collected: f_surface's census row carries 930 double
            multiplies and 775 other double calls, at the bit engine's 558
            cycles, in a stage of 8,447 us -- 38% of a stage the user sees as
            the same waving sheet.  They are include/gl/math.h's double series,
            reached because the lattice hash's argument is far outside 4*pi and
            the reduction that follows is double.  Cheapest rung: keep the
            reduction single-precision for the arguments this path actually
            passes, which is a math.h question with the differential sweep in
            verify/tests/test_gl_math.c as its gate, and it is worth ~2.3x on
            f_surface and 7% on the frame.  Smaller than 12f and independent of
            it; take it after, not instead.

            Re-read 2026-10-14 on the landed 12f image and without the float
            census (`PS2_FP_COUNT=0`, the same run as 12f's verdict): the stage
            is 5,756 us on the steady frame and 8,263 on frame 1, which pays the
            lattice build -- the run's own hash rows read "160 of 8192 computed"
            on frame 1 and "160 of 16384" on frame 2, so 5,756 is the number the
            lane is worth.  The stage shrank by 32% since this cell was priced
            without the doubles' count being re-measured, so the 3,225 us they
            carry is now a LARGER share of the stage than the 38% above: if it is
            still 3,225 us, removing it is 5.76 -> 2.53 ms, 13% of today's frame
            rather than 7% of the frame this cell was written against.  PROVEN:
            the stage's cost.  UNMEASURED: the doubles' share inside it, which
            needs one `PS2_FP_COUNT=1` run, and that run is this lane's first
            rung rather than an assumption in it.  It is still the smaller of the
            two remaining stage levers: LANE 12h is 11.4 ms of the same frame,
            and its deciding row costs one bench run too.

            CLOSED as REFUTED for the steady frame, 2026-10-15, by that rung:
            one `PS2_FP_COUNT=1` bench run on the landed-12f image.  The
            census's `f_surface` row reads dmul=930 dgen=775 D=1,705 on FRAME
            1 -- 8,462 us, the lattice-build frame -- and dmul=0 dgen=0 D=0 on
            the steady frame at 5,815 us.  The instrument proves itself alive
            on the same run: `f_clear` carries D=23 in both frames, and frame
            1's 2,647 us excess over steady matches the bit-engine price of
            1,705 calls, so the zero is a measurement, not an absent flag.
            PROVEN: the steady frame's surface stage spends no double call at
            all.  The ~2.3x this cell priced is already paid -- by the
            large-argument rung and the far-path reduction on COP1 that landed
            after this cell was written -- and no math.h rung is left here.
            What survives is a one-time boot cost of ~3.2 ms on frame 1, which
            belongs to no per-frame budget the wave is judged by.  LANE 12g is
            closed; the remaining stage lever this cell named is LANE 12h's.

  LANE 12h  the menu's 1,436 textured vertices, 11,441 us -- the same walk, the
            same price, the next mesh.  LANE 12f's cell was "stop paying for
            generality where the data is retained", and the frame's census now
            names where the same sentence still applies: `f_menu` is 47% of the
            XMB frame at 2,355 cycles a vertex (11,441 us x 295 over 1,436),
            against the 523 the batch entry costs for the sheet.  It is
            `xb_draw_title()` + `xb_draw_band()` + `xb_draw_items()` +
            `xb_draw_footer()` + `xb_draw_message()`: 646 triangles, 1,436
            vertices, 46,440 px, `0 us fill` and `11,460 us geom` in the raster
            row -- every cycle of it is the immediate-mode walk, and the GS
            rasterises the pixels for nothing, which is the exact shape 12f was
            opened for.  The bar is the same 6.2x, because the vertex count is
            the same order and the walk is the same code: 11.4 ms -> ~1.8 ms,
            47% -> 8% of the frame, and the frame to ~14.7 ms.

            Two rungs, and the first measurement decides between them.
            (i) A textured batch entry: the map refuses on
                `texture_2d_enabled` because a textured vertex carries `s`/`t`
                this map does not compute, so the rung is a second map that does
                -- glyph quads are the app's own atlas rects (font sheet plus
                icon atlas), the same four corners per cell, and the recorder
                already knows how to emit a `TEX0`/`TEX1`/`CLAMP` switch group;
                the live frame carries 22 of them (`texsw=`), and the app's own
                `glBindTexture` sites in the five menu draws are where they come
                from.  Its gate is 12f's differential sweep with `s`/`t` added
                and the refusal codes extended, and its risk is the one this
                lane has already been paid twice for: a textured batch must
                reproduce the texel coordinates the walk's `zp.s`/`zp.t` division
                by the upload's fixed-point scale produces, or the glyphs sample
                the wrong texels, which is visible and not a log row.
            (ii) A replay: the menu's geometry is invariant between frames while
                nothing is hovered and no tween runs -- the labels, the row
                positions and the icon cells are functions of state, not of
                `dt` -- so the stream it produced last frame is a byte string
                this frame could re-send instead of re-deriving, and the send is
                the `qw=` the recorder already holds (15,869 qw a frame total,
                of which the menu's are 1,436 vertices' worth).  Its bound is
                the transfer, not the arithmetic: the GIF upload of a full frame
                costs 4,653 us, so a replay of the menu's span costs a memcpy of
                its quadwords plus its share of that.  Its risk is that the
                recorder's group table is per-frame by law (LANE 10's buffer
                lifetime, restated at 12f's head), so a replay is a second
                lifetime question, not a free copy.
            The deciding measurement is one row and it is cheap: how many of the
            menu's recorded quadwords are byte-identical between two consecutive
            frames of the live wave.  If the answer is "all of them", (ii) is the
            cheaper lane by a factor of the vertex price and (i) is not worth a
            textured map at all.  If it is "not all", (i) is the rung, because
            523 cycles a vertex is already measured.  Take it before 12g, which
            is 5.8 ms of the frame against this lane's 11.4 ms and is
            independent of it either way.

            The instrument, as built (dial `PS2_IDENT=1`, 2026-10-15).  It rides
            the staging sink, which is the one place a frame's groups exist as
            bytes: every quadword a group copies is folded into that group's
            64-bit hash -- two 32-bit FNV lanes, one multiply per lane per word,
            inside the copy loop that already touches the word -- and the table
            of hashes is carried across the frame boundary, because LANE 10's
            buffer-lifetime law says the words themselves may not be.  At the
            next presented frame the two tables are compared BY INDEX and the
            answer prints as `[GSTID]`: `sameg=`/`sameqw=` the groups and
            quadwords identical to the previous frame's same-index group,
            `diffg=`/`diffqw=` the ones that moved, `tailg=`/`tailqw=` the groups
            only one of the two frames has, `at=` the indices that differ (up to
            eight, then `+n`), and `frames=` which presentation this is -- `n=1`
            has no predecessor and is not evidence.  The comparison is positional
            on purpose: a replay can only re-send a span that sits at the same
            index, so an insertion that shifts every later group is exactly the
            cost rung (ii) would have to pay, and it shows up as a frame whose
            whole tail reads different.  Its error is one-directional and that is
            why it is usable as a decision: identical bytes always hash identical,
            so a `sameqw=` claim can only be wrong by a 2^-64 collision, while a
            `diffqw=` claim is exact.  The group count is attributed to a stage by
            the `g=` column the same dial adds to the `[XMBT]` phase rows -- the
            groups staged at each boundary, so the menu's range is the span between
            its two markers and the row's `at=` indices name which stage moved
            without this file holding a second model of the stream.  Cost: it is
            bench-gated (the shipped image compiles none of it) and on the live
            frame it is one multiply pair per quadword, so an ident run's
            microseconds are slightly inflated and only its invariance columns
            are read -- the same rule `PS2_FP_COUNT` states for its counters.
            The host gate is section 7 of `verify/tests/test_eegs_present.c`, 136
            checks, and its controls are the deletions that break a check.  Measured one
            edit at a time on 2026-10-15: not folding the group's words fails 19 checks,
            letting the compare run past the aligned prefix fails 6, not counting the tail
            fails 3 (an inserted group is exactly the cost a replay pays and must not look
            free), letting a frame that staged nothing consume its predecessor fails 2.
            The register lanes: reading the mapping from quadword 0 rather than 1, the
            natural error now that the tag is known to share its quadword with the REGS
            list, fails 7; dropping the report-side vertex-group gate fails the 4
            AD-exclusion checks; folding PRIM into a lane fails the 1 check that IS the
            residual reading; folding absolute coordinates into `shape=`, or folding the
            first vertex along with the rest, fails the same 2 translation checks;
            counting AD groups into `v=` fails 1; zeroing the stored length field fails
            the 4 checks that print `same=`/`diff=`/`tail=` in quadwords.
            Two terms of the instrument are, on this evidence, defensive rather than
            load-bearing: deleting the `qwc` fold, deleting the explicit `qwc` comparison
            in `same`, or deleting both fails nothing -- a group with one more vertex
            folds one more quadword and moves its tag's NLOOP field with it, so the length
            is not a detection term but the field the quadword columns are summed from.
            The sink's own kind gate is redundant the same way, the report-side gate doing
            the exclusion.  An earlier draft of this paragraph claimed a length-only
            control that would call two frames identical; no such pair can be built out of
            real groups, the control measured 0 failures twice over, and the claim is
            deleted rather than kept.

            The row carries one more set of columns than the question strictly
            needs, because a `diffqw=` that says "not replayable" without saying
            WHAT moved would send rung (i) out to design a map for the wrong field.
            Each group also folds per-register lanes: the vertex group's REGS list
            is PRIM, RGBAQ, ST, XYZ2 in that order, its tag occupying ONE quadword
            with the REGS list in its half above, so the body starts at quadword 1
            and a field is `(quadword - 1) mod 4`.  The row prints, over the aligned
            prefix, `xyz=`/`rgb=`/`st=` -- how many groups' drawing kick, colour and
            texel coordinates were each invariant on their own -- plus `shape=`,
            which folds not the coordinates but the DIFFERENCE between consecutive
            vertices of the group (dx, dy, dz of each XYZ2 over the one before, the
            first vertex folding nothing), so a group is `shape=`-invariant exactly
            when it is a rigid translation of last frame's same-index group.  `v=`
            on the same row is the frame's vertex-group count, and it is what makes
            the four lane columns arithmetic rather than inference: `g=` - `v=` is
            the AD groups, and a lane count against `v=` says how many of the
            frame's meshes kept that field.  PRIM and the tag are folded into the
            whole-group hash and into no lane, which leaves a residual that means
            something on its own: a group invariant in all lanes yet counted by
            `diff=` moved its primitive descriptor or its loop count, not its
            picture.  Texture-switch groups carry register writes rather than a
            vertex body and are counted in none of the four, because "the switch
            words are unchanged" is not a fact about any mesh.
            The lanes are per-field views of the same bytes, so they can disagree
            with the whole-group verdict and that is the information, not a defect:
            a group counts in `xyz=` while still differing overall because its
            colour moved.  Cost: one further multiply per quadword per lane on top
            of the pair the group hash already takes, all of it inside the dial.

            What it measured (the run of 2026-10-15, `build/ps2-bench-lanes.log`, the two comparisons
            the bench allows -- frames n=2 and n=3 -- against n=1's baseline).  The live XMB frame
            stages 47 groups, `v=25` of them vertex groups and the other 22 texture switches, and
            15,869 quadwords.  On the steady frame 25 groups and 775 quadwords were byte-identical to
            the previous frame's same-index groups and 22 groups -- 15,094 quadwords, 95 per cent of
            the frame -- differed; the differing indices begin at 1 and end at 46, and the arithmetic
            below shows they are 22 of the frame's 25 MESHES -- the ribbon's splits and the menu's
            cells -- read against the `[XMBT]` `g=` markers (f_bg/f_particles leave 0, the ribbon
            closes 2, the menu's markers reach 46).  The lanes decompose that frame, and the
            decomposition is arithmetic rather than reading: `same=25` and `diff=22` on their own leave
            open how many of the 25 identical groups are switches, but `xyz=3` closes it, because an
            identical group is identical in its lanes too -- so the identical meshes number at most
            three, and three forces the rest.  ALL 22 texture switches replayed byte-for-byte, 3 of the
            25 meshes replayed whole, and the other 22 differ in position (`xyz` credits only the three)
            AND in colour (`rgb` the same three), while `st=25` says not one texel coordinate moved
            anywhere in the frame.  `shape=12` is those three whole replays plus nine rigid
            TRANSLATIONS -- the same mesh in a new place -- so 13 of the 25 meshes changed shape.
            Frame 3 closes the same way at `xyz=2`: 22 switches and two meshes replayed, 23
            meshes differ, `shape=2` is only the two whole replays so that frame translated
            NOTHING, and `st=24` names the one mesh that re-bound its texels.  Two comparisons
            is the bench's sample (it ends the session two frames past the budget), so the
            translation count is a bounded observation, not a measured rate.
            The lane's verdict, therefore, in its own terms.  Rung (ii), the replay, is REFUTED as
            stated: what is invariant is the 22 texture switches and the handful of groups `same=`
            counts, 5 per cent of the frame's bytes, and a replay of that span would be a memcpy of
            775 quadwords in a lane whose whole cost is 15,869.  Rung (i) is the lane, and the columns
            name its shape.  A textured batch map does not have to compute `s`/`t` at all -- the texel
            coordinates are per-mesh constants the app already carries, which is what `st=` is for
            every frame -- while it does have to re-emit position AND colour every frame.  And the
            cheap version of that map, a retained mesh plus a per-group offset, is not carried by the
            measurement: `shape=` was nonzero on one frame and 2 on the next, so the menu's meshes are
            re-solved rather than moved, and an offset-only batch would have to re-derive the shape
            anyway.  Where the work therefore is: the app's own per-vertex position and colour for
            twenty menu groups and the ribbon's two, at the 523 cycles a vertex this lane already
            prices -- which is rung (i) as 12f's gate left it, with the texel term now known to be
            free.  If a future run prints `shape=` at `v=` and `xyz=` below it for frame after frame,
            that is the fact that would reopen the offset-only idea, and this paragraph is the place
            it gets corrected.

            Run (i) as it will be built, amended 2026-10-15 before the code, so the code that
            disagrees with this has a text to correct against.  Three rungs, each one an extension
            of a thing that already exists, and one gloss on `st=` that the sentence above needs:
            the texel term is free in the sense that no MAP derives it -- the app hands the batch
            the same `u`/`v` it hands `glTexCoord2f` today -- not in the sense that the stream
            carries no ST: every textured vertex the GS draws still gets its texel float, and the
            batch must produce it through the walk's own two-step chain or the glyphs sample the
            wrong texels.  That chain is `zp.s = TGL_TRUNC(u*sscale + soff)`
            (`vertex_stage.c:92`, the mapper's constants from `texture.c:172-175`) and then
            `gs_float_scale_pow2((float)zp.s, GS_TEX_S_SHIFT)` (`clip.c:670`; S_SHIFT 11, T_SHIFT
            19, the fraction bits being 10 and 18), and reproducing it bit-for-bit IS the gate.

            (a) A textured map: `tgl_grid_map_tex(tgl_tex_map_t *)` in `grid_map.h` and
                `vertex_stage.c`, its `tgl_tex_map_t` carrying a whole `tgl_grid_map_t` -- the same
                position lift, the same refusals STALE/LIGHT/W/SKEW, the same rect, `smooth` and
                `blended` -- plus the bound image's four mapper constants and its `pixmap` as an
                opaque plane key.  The one inversion is the TEXTURE condition: the grey map refuses
                on `texture_2d_enabled` because it cannot say `s`/`t`; the textured map REQUIRES
                `texture_2d_enabled && current_texture != NULL && images[0].pixmap != NULL` and
                refuses with a new code, `TGL_GRID_REFUSE_NOTEX 10`, without it.  Requires rather
                than assumes, because the walk's own arm (`clip.c:754-761`) draws a bound-but-
                never-uploaded sheet FLAT, with `zp.s`/`zp.t` left stale and unread; a batch that
                took that state would be claiming a plane the hardware was never given.  A refusal
                is not a loss there: the caller's fallback is the walk, which draws the same flat
                primitive.
            (b) A textured points map: `tgl_grid_map_tex_points(m, xs, ys, us, vs, cols, n, out)`,
                the colours four interleaved floats a vertex (an icon quad's (br,br,br,a) and its
                shadow's (0,0,0,a) are the general per-channel case of the walk's colour line, and
                `grid_channel_byte` is already that case's truncate-and-mask, byte-identical to
                `gs_vertex`'s `col->r >> 16`), the points `tgl_tex_pt_t {x_px, y_px, rgba, s, t}`
                -- `eegs_vertex_t`'s own shape, which is the emission the batch replaces.  The S/T
                loop is the two-step chain above, and both steps are host-compilable in this unit:
                the scale is integer arithmetic on a float's bits whose sweep is
                `make test-ps2-gs-pack`, and the int-to-float is `cvt.s.w`, which this CPU answers.
                The floats are stored into memory, never returned -- and the loops keep 12f's split
                shape for the same reason: past the caller-saved singles the o32 ABI has, live FPR
                pairs leave through `sdc1`/`ldc1`, which is the silent-garbage class TEST 66 greps
                the linked image for.
            (c) A backend entry: `eegs_tex_quads(m, pts, nquads)` in `backend_eegs.c`, gating
                exactly as `eegs_grid_strip` gates -- refusal before anything is recorded, the
                `ok`-carries-`refuse` route, the XYZ2 RANGE gate with `over=` counting what the
                SCISSOR is asked to cut, the same `batch=`/`slow=`/`why=`/`over=` columns because
                it is one mesh, one decision, one set of counters either way.  Then
                `eegs_texture(gs_tex_find(m->pixmap))` ONCE for the entry, not once per triangle
                (the registry lookup lives here, in the stage that owns the GS's vocabulary, not in
                `vertex_stage.c` which must not learn about drivers), and then each quad split
                (v0,v1,v2) and (v0,v2,v3) -- `vertex.c:270-278`'s own GL_QUADS split -- through the
                existing `eegs_triangle()`, whose `select_prim` word compare is what keeps a run of
                same-state quads inside one PRIM group exactly as the menu's triangles are kept
                today.  A plane that never got a VRAM address demotes through `need_plane` and
                counts `texmiss`, identically to the walk.
            (d) Call sites: `xb_draw_icon()`'s shadow-and-main pair (8 points) and
                `xb_draw_text_run()`'s glyph row (4 a glyph, the run's shadow pass separate from
                its main pass), each asked INSIDE its own `glBegin`, because 12f's rung (iii)
                measured that the model-projection product is only this window's once `glopBegin`
                has multiplied the stacks; the immediate-mode walk stays in the file verbatim as
                the refusal route, as it does for the ribbon.  `xb_draw_bg`'s five gradient
                triangles and the message box's two flat shapes are untextured or off the measured
                stage; they stay on the walk.
            (e) The gate is 12f's differential sweep with `s`/`t` added and the refusal codes
                extended: a real uploaded sheet (so the mapper's constants are `texture.c`'s own,
                not invented), TEXTURE_2D enabled, vertices run through
                `tgl_vertex_transform` + `tgl_vertex_to_viewport` + the same inline scale `gs_vertex`
                applies, compared bit-for-bit against the batch's `x_px`/`y_px`/`rgba`/`s`/`t`; the
                refusals swept at texture-off, no-binding, bound-never-uploaded (NOTEX) and a stale
                product (STALE).  The recorder half -- the quad stream decoding to the same triangle
                groups the list path emitted -- is `test_eegs_raster`'s question, asked of the new
                entry.
            The bar is the cell's: 11,441 us at 2,355 cycles a vertex toward ~1.8 ms at the ~523
            the batch entry already prices, 47% of the frame toward 8%.  The risk is unchanged and
            the book has named it twice now: a wrong texel coordinate is VISIBLE and not a log row,
            so the visual half of the verdict is the user's and stays SKIP until it is given.

            What run (i) measured (the eegs bench run of 2026-10-15, `build/ps2-bench.log`, dials
            `PS2_GL=eegs PS2_FP=hw`, ident off, the steady frame read against the same-dial
            baseline in `build/ps2-bench.prev.log`).  The entry took the whole menu.  `batch=`
            reads 64 -- the ribbon's strip and sixty-three textured quad entries, each icon's
            shadow-and-main ask and each text run's two passes its own entry -- with `slow=0` and
            `why=0`: no refusal anywhere in the session, and `texmiss=0`: no batched vertex
            demoted to flat.  The census prints no `raster f_menu` row at all, where the baseline
            printed 646 tris and 1,436 verts: with the entry open the menu's walk ran ZERO
            triangles, every corner of every glyph and icon left through the batch.  The stream
            paid for it in the open: `groups=` 47 -> 51 (two vertex groups and two switch groups,
            the planes the entries bind at their head), `verts=` 3,937 -> 4,153, `qw=` 15,869 ->
            16,743 -- the +874 is 216 vertices x 4 body words plus the two tags plus the two
            switch groups' four words each, so the quadword column closes on the vertex column
            exactly.  `over=` rose 32 -> 184 because the batched menu hands its off-rect corners
            to the SCISSOR rather than to the clipper; the refusal column stayed empty, which is
            the difference between those two numbers.

            The stage then cost 6,633 us against the baseline's 11,441 -- 1.73x -- and the cell's
            bar of ~1.8 ms at 6.2x is retired here, by arithmetic this file can now check.  The
            bar multiplied the walk's 1,436 vertices by the batch's measured 523 cycles and
            priced nothing else; a GL_QUADS leaves through `vertex.c:270-278`'s own split as SIX
            stream vertices per quad -- the split is what the host gate asserts, two quads, twelve
            vertices, four triangles -- and a batched menu therefore pays emission at the entry's
            fine price for MORE vertices than the walk's corners counted, not fewer.  6.6 ms is
            what the stage costs with zero walk triangles in it: the entry plus the app's own
            per-quad loop (`xb_font_uv`, the fill's float writes), a split this run does not
            partition -- the column that would name it is one more clock pair inside the call
            sites, and that is this cell's next rung.  The watched loop, in the frame's own
            columns: steady `paint=` 24,343 -> 19,487 us (1.25x), `pass=` 67,934 -> 58,643,
            `period=` 83,944 -> 74,653 (1.12x); the folded cadence bounds read `frame 10` at
            769 ms baseline against 717 ms now.  And the composite's LANE 12i residue -- the
            9,011 us that used to be unnamed -- halved to 4,578 with the menu, its `ovl=` column
            now naming 4,493 of it directly with `rst=` at 85: 12i named the residue as an open
            menu's, and 12h cutting it by half is the same fact read by a different column on the
            same frames.

            The gate behind it, as built.  `test_eegs_raster` is 277 checks and
            `test_eegs_present` 136, both green at this writing.  The differential half runs the
            batch against the walk's own two `eegs_triangle` calls per quad and compares the
            decoded stream corner-set-for-corner-set on `x_px`/`y_px`/`rgba` and the two-step
            texel chain, byte-for-byte; the alternate-diagonal split is the control that fires
            INSIDE the green run -- a decoder that could not tell the two diagonals apart would be
            approving the wrong quads -- and the gates half sweeps ARG, UNSET, NOTEX and STALE
            refusals, the RANGE refusal's recorded-first law (a pre-recorded triangle survives,
            the refused run commits nothing, `over=` stays 0), the overhang-into-SCISSOR route,
            and the bound-never-uploaded demotion through `need_plane` with `texmiss` counting
            what the flat draw owes.  The registry collision is settled per suite, not globally:
            `ps2_gs_tex.c` and `ps2_gs_rec.c` both own file-static `s_n`/`s_sink`, so no TU can
            include both, and the suites stand a `gs_tex_find` up locally -- pointer identity
            against one test plane where the pairing IS the subject, an always-0 registry where it
            is not, which is the same answer the machine gives before any plane is uploaded.  The
            visual half stays the user's: the host gate proves the texel WORDS are the ones the
            walk would have sent, and only eyes prove they land on the right glyph.  A future run
            that prints `raster f_menu` again, or `slow=` above zero, or `texmiss=` above zero on
            the live wave, means an entry opened that this paragraph counted as closed, and this
            is the paragraph corrected.

  LANE 12i  the composite's unnamed 9,011 us -- an open menu, drawn twice.
            The partition LANE 12f's landing cell wrote down carries exactly one
            term no instrument owns: `render 63,267 = comp total 54,256 + 9,011
            unnamed`.  It is 13.3% of the pass and 10.7% of the period, and the
            re-read bar leaves the remaining 2x in this residue and in `frame=`,
            so naming it is the rung.  Two runs already on disk say WHERE it
            lives before any new row is printed, and they say it with one column.
            The shipped-dial census (build/ps2-bench.prev.log) and the ident run
            (build/ps2-bench-lanes.log) each print three app frames; read
            `render - total` against `why=`:

              why=d  why=c  why=1   ->  9,021 us   9,011 us      88 us
              why=d  why=c  why=1   ->  9,111 us   9,131 us     219 us

            (bit 4 is `global_menu_is_open()`, bit 8 is
            `tracker_is_menu_open()`.)  The compositor's own columns say the two
            9 ms frames are the same desktop being composited -- `bg=` 7,509
            against 7,499, `frame=` 7,877 against 7,869, `blit=` 12,713 against
            12,671, `panel=` 1,587 against 1,568, `bars=` 310 against 296, each
            inside 1% of the other; the sixth, `paint=`, is the app's and moves
            with its animation phase -- and only the frames that carry an overlay
            bit owe the residue.  PROVEN: the residue is a property of an open
            menu, not of the app's frame.

            The code says which block, and it needs no new instrument to say it.
            The render timer's bracket is `ps2_paint_region()`
            (src/cores/core_ps2.c:1228): t0, `eegs_present_begin()`, the
            composite, `eegs_present_end()`, t1.  The composite is the ONLY step
            the compositor times -- `comp total` closes `t_all` inside
            `redraw_baremetal_desktop*()` (src/desktop/desktop.c:418 and :485) --
            and on the whole-canvas branch the composite is `workbench_render()`
            (src/desktop/workbench.c:86), whose last FOUR steps run after that
            window has closed: `global_menu_render_overlay()`,
            `tracker_render_menu()`, `workbench_cover_overlays()` and the cursor.
            So the residue is that block plus the recorder's bracket, and what is
            missing is the split.  The reap is already exonerated by a column
            that exists: [GSTS] gif's `max=87 us` is the longest single wait the
            ring's reap took all session, so a 9 ms residue is not the GIF.

            The same bits move a second column, and that one says the open menu
            is not only repainted but re-shipped.  `upload=` reads 4,648 and
            4,653 us on the overlay frames and 922 us on the frame carrying
            neither bit, because `workbench_cover_overlays()` hands the open
            menu's rect to the cover list -- the canvas content re-uploaded OVER
            the stream, which LANE 9d refused under the stream and never refused
            over it.  An open item list therefore costs roughly 9.0 ms of canvas
            and 3.7 ms of upload: 12.7 ms of the 67,934 us pass, on the frames
            the user watches while the launcher's list is up.

            The instrument is one column on the compositor's own idiom, and it
            prints its own control.  A seventh stat_max, `overlay_us`, timed
            across `workbench_render()`'s post-composite block and printed as
            `ovl=` beside the six, with `rst=` = `render - total - ovl` printed
            next to it: the identity has to close to the 88-219 us the no-overlay
            frames already leave, because the block is timed where the port
            cannot see it and the recorder's bracket is what the port cannot
